Appendix C.  Design Context and Boundaries

   This appendix records what this document leaves to other work and
   explains one design choice: how a delegated token identifies its
   client.

C.1.  Scope

   This document is self-contained: it binds Missions to OAuth 2.0 and
   depends normatively only on the specifications listed as its
   normative references.

   Its reference to the OAuth Actor Profile
   ([I-D.draft-mcguinness-oauth-actor-profile]), for the act chain shape
   the optional Delegation capability uses, is normative and binds only
   a deployment that claims Delegation, so the mandatory single-domain
   core does not depend on it.

   The companion Mission Cross-Domain Projection profile
   [I-D.draft-mcguinness-oauth-mission-cross-domain] specifies cross-
   domain projection, a single hop that lets an authorization server in
   another trust domain honor a Mission, and carries the identity-
   chaining and Identity Assertion JWT Authorization Grant (ID-JAG)
   dependencies.  The Cross-Domain capability's conformance bar is self-
   contained in this document (Section 15), so that companion is not a
   normative dependency.

   This document defines no mechanism that pins a Mission to an approved
   agent deployment class or version, and reserves no Intent member for
   one.  Such a pin needs two objects, not one Intent member: a
   committed approval-context pin, and presenter-instance evidence
   checked at every derivation (for example, using
   [I-D.draft-mcguinness-oauth-client-instance-id] and
   [I-D.draft-mcguinness-oauth-client-attesters]).  A profile that
   defines the pin also defines:

   *  its request carriage and resolution to an approved deployment
      identifier;

   *  its Mission Record extension and approval rendering; and

   *  its fail-closed behavior when the client cannot prove the pin.

   This document defines no cumulative consumption bounds (for example,
   a budget, call-count, or activity-duration cap).  An experimental
   companion ([I-D.draft-mcguinness-mission-metering]) defines them as
   explicit Mission Intent extension members, together with the runtime
   metering that enforces them.

C.2.  Non-Goals

   The following are out of scope for this document:

   *  *Semantic / intent verification.* This document does not evaluate
      whether a runtime action serves the Mission's purpose beyond
      matching the approved authorization_details and constraints; per-
      action evaluation is the runtime layer's role (Section 16.3.1).
      Verifying an agent's declared reasoning against the task is a
      further attestation problem outside both layers.

   *  *Joint outcome and cross-resource effects.* Whether the actions
      taken under a Mission jointly accomplish its task is out of scope,
      as are the ordering, timing, atomicity, and compensation of their
      effects across resource servers.  The mission claim joins requests
      to an approval for issuance and audit; it does not identify a
      business transaction.

   *  *Approval-free authorization upgrade.* The Authority Set is
      committed at approval, and this document defines no mid-stream
      widening that bypasses consent.  Widening requires a new approval,
      a successor Mission, as specified by Mission Expansion
      [I-D.draft-mcguinness-oauth-mission-expansion].

   *  *Lifecycle event distribution.* A resource server learns Mission
      state from the token lifetime or optional introspection
      (Section 9.2); this document defines no push-based notification of
      Mission state changes.  Mission Lifecycle Signals
      ([I-D.draft-mcguinness-oauth-mission-signals]) specifies a Shared
      Signals and CAEP profile for Mission lifecycle events, delivered
      as Security Event Tokens ([RFC8935]).

   *  *Human-in-the-loop suspension.* The base lifecycle is active and
      terminated (Section 10).  Mission Status
      ([I-D.draft-mcguinness-oauth-mission-status]) defines a suspended
      state with resume/complete transitions as an optional extension.
      A pending-human-approval state and a holding-token pause-and-
      resume protocol are future lifecycle work.

   *  *Multi-hop cross-domain projection.* Cross-Domain Projection
      ([I-D.draft-mcguinness-oauth-mission-cross-domain]) specifies a
      single projection hop.  Mission Cross-Organizational Delegation
      ([I-D.draft-mcguinness-oauth-mission-cross-org-delegation])
      profiles recursive delegation across organizations; a destination
      verifies that delegation chain before projecting.  Re-projecting a
      Mission from one Resource AS into a further domain is future work.

   *  *Decentralized agent identity.* Agent identity and credentialing
      are out of scope ([I-D.draft-ietf-wimse-aims] and the WIMSE
      architecture, [I-D.draft-ietf-wimse-arch]); this document governs
      the approved-task artifact those identities act within, not the
      identities themselves.

   *  *Cross-audience unlinkability.* A single canonical Mission
      Identifier lets any party holding a token correlate a Mission's
      activity across audiences and resources. authority_hash is not
      part of that baseline correlation surface: it stays on the Mission
      Record and the audit and profile surfaces that carry it by
      disclosure privilege (Section 7.8, Section 9.2.1), not on every
      token by default.  Pairwise or unlinkable presentation of Mission-
      bound authority works against the identifier and is therefore
      future work (Section 17.1).

C.3.  Client Identification in Delegated Tokens

   This document keeps the registered meaning of the client_id claim,
   stated normatively in Section 7.2 and enforced in Section 9.1: the
   OAuth client that requested the token, on every issued or derived
   token, a delegated one included.  Downstream delegates are named in
   the act chain (Section 8.2), and the Mission's originally-approved
   agent remains recorded in the Mission Record (Section 6.3).

   The alternative, fixing client_id to the approved agent on every
   derived token, would lead a generic [RFC9068] resource server or
   logging pipeline to misattribute a delegate's action to the approved
   agent, with no error to surface the mismatch.  It would be safe only
   where every resource server already processes the act chain, and
   Mission awareness alone does not establish that (Section 9.1.2).

   The routing rule of Section 9.1 does not depend on this choice.  The
   mission claim's presence signals that a token may carry an act chain
   a consumer needs to process.  A Mission-unaware resource server
   cannot opt into that processing, so routing a delegated token to one
   is forbidden.

Appendix D.  Integrity Anchor Test Vectors

   These non-normative vectors let an implementation verify its anchor
   computation (Section 6.4.1, Section 6.4.2) byte for byte.  All use
   the issuer https://as.example.com.

   Each canonical-bytes block is the exact JCS [RFC8785] output: a
   single line of UTF-8 with no whitespace outside string values.  It is
   wrapped here for layout only; removing the line breaks, and adding no
   characters, recovers the canonical form.

   JCS sorts object member names and preserves array order.  In these
   vectors, iss precedes typ precedes value; within an entry, actions
   precedes constraints precedes resource precedes type; and within
   max_amount, amount precedes currency.

   intent_hash, over this Mission Intent as the envelope value with typ
   mission-intent:

   {
     "goal": "Reconcile Q3 invoices",
     "target_resources": ["https://erp.example.com"],
     "expires_at": "2026-12-31T23:59:59Z"
   }

   Canonical bytes of the envelope:

   {"iss":"https://as.example.com","typ":"mission-intent","value":{"e
   xpires_at":"2026-12-31T23:59:59Z","goal":"Reconcile Q3 invoices","
   target_resources":["https://erp.example.com"]}}

   intent_hash = sha-256:sE_2V3NaDpGNYM8dH1tLpNJnj-RmaHN3FC6ZcbOLJSw

   authority_hash, over this Authority Set as the envelope value with
   typ mission-authority-set:

   [
     { "type": "mission_resource_access",
       "resource": "https://erp.example.com",
       "actions": ["invoices.read"] },
     { "type": "mission_resource_access",
       "resource": "https://erp.example.com",
       "actions": ["journal-entries.write"],
       "constraints": {
         "max_amount": { "amount": "500.00", "currency": "USD" }
       } }
   ]

   Canonical bytes of the envelope:

   {"iss":"https://as.example.com","typ":"mission-authority-set","val
   ue":[{"actions":["invoices.read"],"resource":"https://erp.example.
   com","type":"mission_resource_access"},{"actions":["journal-entrie
   s.write"],"constraints":{"max_amount":{"amount":"500.00","currency
   ":"USD"}},"resource":"https://erp.example.com","type":"mission_res
   ource_access"}]}

   authority_hash = sha-256:vUCCfjGulit9u0qJ0Z6pQSNerZtXMqRlfJNCr4PzLro

   The next two vectors exercise an additional flat Intent member beyond
   target_resources, and an Authority Set entry whose
   delegation.allowed_delegates is an array of matcher objects.  JCS
   preserves the array's order, so the sub_profile matcher stays before
   the sub matcher.

   intent_hash, over this Mission Intent as the envelope value with typ
   mission-intent:

   {
     "goal": "Reconcile Q3 invoices",
     "target_resources": ["https://erp.example.com"],
     "expires_at": "2026-12-31T23:59:59Z",
     "purpose": "urn:example:purpose:reconcile"
   }

   Canonical bytes of the envelope:

   {"iss":"https://as.example.com","typ":"mission-intent","value":{"e
   xpires_at":"2026-12-31T23:59:59Z","goal":"Reconcile Q3 invoices","
   purpose":"urn:example:purpose:reconcile","target_resources":["http
   s://erp.example.com"]}}

   intent_hash = sha-256:ug7xNsun-TbvBCr-_uFP74-CBEs8pwlPmD-doEyKDu8

   authority_hash, over this Authority Set as the envelope value with
   typ mission-authority-set:

   [
     { "type": "mission_resource_access",
       "resource": "https://erp.example.com",
       "actions": ["invoices.read"],
       "delegation": {
         "max_depth": 2,
         "allowed_delegates": [
           { "sub_profile": "ai_agent" },
           { "sub": "s6BhdRkqt3" }
         ]
       } }
   ]

   Canonical bytes of the envelope:

   {"iss":"https://as.example.com","typ":"mission-authority-set","val
   ue":[{"actions":["invoices.read"],"delegation":{"allowed_delegates
   ":[{"sub_profile":"ai_agent"},{"sub":"s6BhdRkqt3"}],"max_depth":2}
   ,"resource":"https://erp.example.com","type":"mission_resource_acc
   ess"}]}

   authority_hash = sha-256:notrA9wZaP3I5Gx8UzN0mfzUjHYPeX4Ri_B3ilh7BbA

   The next vector exercises the third anchor: proposal_hash, over this
   submitted authorization_details proposal as the envelope value with
   typ mission-proposed-authority (Section 4.3):

   [
     { "type": "mission_resource_access",
       "resource": "https://erp.example.com",
       "actions": ["invoices.*"] },
     { "type": "mission_resource_access",
       "resource": "https://erp.example.com",
       "actions": ["journal-entries.write"],
       "constraints": {
         "max_amount": { "amount": "1000.00", "currency": "USD" }
       } }
   ]

   Canonical bytes of the envelope:

   {"iss":"https://as.example.com","typ":"mission-proposed-authority"
   ,"value":[{"actions":["invoices.*"],"resource":"https://erp.exampl
   e.com","type":"mission_resource_access"},{"actions":["journal-entr
   ies.write"],"constraints":{"max_amount":{"amount":"1000.00","curre
   ncy":"USD"}},"resource":"https://erp.example.com","type":"mission_
   resource_access"}]}

   proposal_hash = sha-256:udzftXYQy0pvYNxz4KgtmyL_EV8ry4DhIbBFfwILEBA

   The last vector is the entry commitment (Section 6.4.1), computed
   over one immutable Mission-record Authority Set entry, not an issued
   or narrowed token projection.

   The entry commitment, over this entry as the envelope value with typ
   mission-authority-entry:

   {
     "type": "mission_resource_access",
     "resource": "https://erp.example.com",
     "actions": ["invoices.read"],
     "constraints": {
       "resource_issued_after": "2026-07-01T00:00:00Z",
       "resource_issued_before": "2026-09-30T23:59:59Z"
     },
     "delegation": {
       "max_depth": 2,
       "allowed_delegates": [{ "sub_profile": "ai_agent" }]
     }
   }

   Canonical bytes of the envelope:

   {"iss":"https://as.example.com","typ":"mission-authority-entry","v
   alue":{"actions":["invoices.read"],"constraints":{"resource_issued
   _after":"2026-07-01T00:00:00Z","resource_issued_before":"2026-09-
   30T23:59:59Z"},"delegation":{"allowed_delegates":[{"sub_profile":"
   ai_agent"}],"max_depth":2},"resource":"https://erp.example.com","t
   ype":"mission_resource_access"}}

   entry_digest = sha-256:OUrwTnuirT29YxQmMSyiJce8W1PfGryvrVViQ1lJCqQ

   An implementation that canonicalizes the same value under the same
   typ and iss, computes SHA-256, and encodes as sha-256: followed by
   base64url with no padding (Section 6.4.1) reproduces these anchors
   exactly.  A divergence indicates a JCS or encoding difference to
   resolve before interoperating.

Appendix E.  Document History

   [[ To be removed from the final specification ]]

   -02

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Submission via PAR is retitled
      Submitting a Mission Intent, with subsections for the Submission
      envelope, its carriage through PAR, and other carriages.
      Authority Proposal, Preparing the Approval, Approver
      Authentication Strength, and Issuance Gating are restructured into
      subsections, shorter sentences, and lists; numbered steps, checks,
      and rules keep their numbers, and Issuance Gating keeps its
      numbered procedure at its head (#1908, D1042).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: the Introduction states the
      problem and the approach and points to the Overview; it no longer
      restates the flow that Protocol Flow and the Introductory Example
      show, or details stated in their own sections (consent to the
      Authority Set, the commitments, grant binding, revocation by
      identifier, the mission claim and resource-server enforcement).
      The issuance-only deployment pointer moves to Issuance Scope and
      Runtime Enforcement.  Protocol Flow gains a legend keyed to its
      figure that carries the flow's properties, with a pointer to each
      section that specifies them (#1906, D1039).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: the back matter that describes
      the family rather than the protocol moves to the Architecture: the
      Mapping Assessment of this document against the Mission Substrate
      contract, Relationship to Adjacent Work, the two future-work
      items, and the Derivation Policy appendix's policy artifact,
      properties, fixtures, and ownership guidance.  Claiming
      Conformance states only that this document makes no substrate
      conformance claim; the mission claim's distinction from similarly
      named claims follows its JSON Web Token Claims registration; Scope
      and Future Work becomes Scope, keeping its boundary statements;
      and the Derivation Policy appendix keeps its worked example
      (#1794, D973).

   *  Token Claims: a token whose format a companion profile explicitly
      defines, with its own typ and verification rules (the Mission
      Offline Attenuation root), follows that profile; JWT access-token
      validation stays under Section 4 of RFC 9068, accepting at+jwt or
      application/at+jwt (#1760).

   *  Configured-mapping mode can key on the requested scope values for
      a scope-only target, through that target's trusted scope-
      projection mapping, which also relates a requested value back to
      authority (Derivation Modes, Scope Projection).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Standing-Consent Bases points to
      the Architecture for the companion-defined bases instead of
      listing them; Grant Bindings and Related Mechanisms, Composition
      and the Effective Ceiling, Authority Materialized Before
      Containment, and the approval_basis definition no longer restate
      what the companions specify; the sentence on joining one
      credential to different Missions across requests moves to the
      Mission Authority Server's Mission Join.

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Grant Bindings and Related
      Mechanisms places the Template profile's Dispatch Handoff and the
      continuation profile's Identity Continuation Transport, the
      reusable-state case reads as a redeemable grant lineage, the
      derivation resolution list points to profile-defined grants, and a
      derived token's Mission is the one its subject_token resolved to,
      with no binding assumed, and Grant Bindings and Related Mechanisms
      and Delegated Exchange keep a token's backing grant provenance
      where its subject_token was issued under a grant lineage (#1589,
      D726).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Grant Bindings and Related
      Mechanisms names the Token Exchanges its later items cover, and
      the Namespace Taxonomy lists the approval_basis.type values as
      specification-defined, which Standing-Consent Bases cites.

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: the approval_event_id definition
      names the approval event that creates the Mission and states that,
      under a standing-consent basis, it identifies the instance's
      creation, not the earlier standing approval.

   *  An access token's mission claim, and its introspection mission
      member, are reserved for issuance that conforms to a Mission-
      binding profile; a profile that defines another issuance or
      derivation path identifies its grant or chain, authority bound,
      state check, and consumer checks (Claim Reservation).
      Introspection discloses mission only for a token issued on a
      conforming path.  A resource server's mission.issuer check accepts
      the relationship any Mission-binding profile it has adopted
      establishes, not only a cross-domain profile's (Enforcement
      Requirements).

   *  A Mission-governed resource, classified by the deployment or by
      its authenticated mission_bound_authorization_required metadata,
      gets no token outside a Mission on any grant type; the open-ended
      policy exception is removed, and the refusal is invalid_target
      (Authority Proposal).

   *  A token carries only the entries whose original approved audience
      (the audiences the AS's trusted mapping assigned an entry at
      approval, retained with the Mission and kept by the entries a
      derived Mission inherits) includes its audience, unless a declared
      narrowing capability permits issuing an entry there.  A token
      naming several audiences is issued only where each is approved for
      the same entries; otherwise, or where the audience is approved for
      none of the entries the token would carry, the request is refused
      with invalid_target (Declared Transformation Capabilities,
      Audience and Token Response; #1503, D620, D733).

   *  A designation's aud names its redeemer and reassigns no entry's
      original approved audience: the audience rules test the audiences
      associated with the entries it carries, a permitted subset of the
      presented token's that excludes an entry without an original
      approved audience, and the delegated token issued at its
      redemption is subject to every audience rule (Audience and Token
      Response, Declared Transformation Capabilities; #1717, D845,
      D859).

   *  At a delegated exchange the AS authenticates the requesting client
      by a method it relies on to identify the client and refuses any
      other, including every public client, with invalid_client and no
      token; a claimed client_id, the designation, or an independently
      chosen key does not authenticate it.  That client authentication
      alone identifies the delegate: the AS refuses an actor_token from
      an authenticated client with invalid_request.  An exchange
      presenting a designation is a delegated exchange, never a no-actor
      one (Delegated Exchange, Designation, Self-Exchange Down-Scoping,
      Establishing and Resolving a Binding, Error and Challenge Mapping,
      Token Theft, OAuth Binding Mapping Assessment; #1718, D845, D859).

   *  The key-publication requirement applies to an AS that issues
      Mission-bound JWT access tokens for resource servers to consume,
      not to one issuing only designations; the first capability carrier
      that declares a member governs, true or false, and a declaration
      waives no check; the declared narrowing capability also governs
      Authority Set derivation, where without it a candidate entry is
      retained unchanged or omitted; an undecidable token-endpoint
      subset comparison is refused with invalid_authorization_details;
      and short-lived is related to the termination latency a deployment
      accepts, with numerical bounds left to deployment profiles (Token
      Claims, Declared Transformation Capabilities, Authority Proposal,
      Derivation Modes, Subset Rule, Issuance Gating; #1720, D845).

   *  The AS refuses, with invalid_target, a delegated Mission-bound
      token for an audience it has not established as a Mission-aware
      Resource Server that processes the act chain; routing and logging
      beyond that audience stay the deployment's (Mission-Unaware
      Resource Servers, Delegated Exchange; #1503, D620).

   *  Each redemption of a designation validates the originating
      credential and all applicable grant state backing it, which an
      exchange that establishes no new grant lineage keeps; a
      designation's expiry bounds its redemption, not the lifetime of
      the delegated token issued from it (Delegated Exchange,
      Designation, Authentication Claims and Lifetime; #1503, D620).

   *  Step 1 of the Submission Processing Order applies the size bounds
      and checks each evidence entry's structure, so a structurally
      invalid entry is refused with invalid_request before the authority
      proposal is validated, and Conformance Roles names that code for
      it (Submission Processing Order, Intent Submission Evidence,
      Conformance Roles; #1503, #1482, D620).

   *  The deployment's published approval-authentication floor, and the
      mission member of an active introspection response to a caller
      authorized for Mission data, are requirements rather than
      lowercase statements (Approver Authentication Strength, Mission
      State via Token Introspection; #1503, D620).

   *  A Mission is revoked by a party the deployment's policy
      authorizes, or by that policy itself; the Subject, the Approver,
      and an administrator can be authorized, but none is by role alone
      (Revocation, Mission Lifecycle; #1503, D620).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: the Derivation definition, in
      Terminology and Issuance Gating, includes the redemption of a
      profile-defined grant, such as the Template profile's handoff
      grant; a grant binding resolves the Mission for the operations
      using it, and a profile-defined grant can instead resolve it
      through its authenticated mission reference (#1631, D751).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: the Mission Status variation's
      revocation reason describes a revocation, not a completion
      (#1535).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Single Accountable Approver
      states that authenticating approval evidence does not establish
      that conditions governing later reliance remain satisfied, and
      that the effect of later changes to an Approver's role, a
      delegation, or governing policy on an existing Mission is
      deployment policy (#830).

   *  When the AS establishes that a Mission's recorded Subject has been
      deprovisioned, it SHOULD terminate the Mission with reason
      revoked; temporary disablement, an outage, or another principal's
      departure does not trigger the rule, and termination does not
      recall issued tokens (Revocation; #830).

   *  A direct approval whose commit finds the effective expiry passed
      is refused with access_denied, with no Mission or authorization
      code (Committing the Mission).  A token request that fails several
      checks gets any applicable error, subject to the orders this
      document states.  A state a companion defines takes that
      companion's mission_error value, never a termination reason
      (Issuance Gating).  A resource server answers an opaque Mission-
      bound token resolved as inactive with invalid_token, and a
      resolution it cannot complete with HTTP 503 and no token error
      (Introspected Token Consumption).  Enforcement Requirements
      refuses an entry it cannot enforce only with the codes of the
      error table, and Mission awareness alone does not imply act
      processing (Error and Challenge Mapping, Client Identification in
      Delegated Tokens; #1721, D845).

   *  mission_id and mission_expires_at are required on the response
      that first delivers a newly created Mission's identifier or
      credential (on the authorization-code path, the token response,
      not the code-only authorization response) and on a delegated
      exchange response, and recommended on every other Mission-bound
      token response; a client learns them from the response, not from
      the access token.  A client assumes no authority absent from the
      granted authorization_details and need not reject a narrower grant
      (Mission Identifier and Expiry in Responses, Audience and Token
      Response, Conformance Roles; #1719, D845).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: the AS answers a request at its
      final decision to release the result, so a result prepared before
      an acknowledged revocation and released after it is refused
      (Issuance Gating); the Mission Record is a logical object with no
      required storage form; the Authority types extension point lists
      what a type's specification supplies; resource-server conformance
      implies no particular type (Conformance Roles, Authorization
      Details Types); each mission_error value is explained to the
      client (Issuance Gating); and Scope Projection gains an example
      (#1722).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Adjacent Work distinguishes the
      mission claim from AAuth's mission_s256 claim and the WIMSE AIMS
      agent mission; target_resources, like purpose, grants no authority
      as a configured-mapping key; Terminology defines "Mission-governed
      client"; and Issuance Gating gives the reason a Token Exchange
      refusal uses invalid_request (#1532).

   *  The consent rendering attributes client-supplied text to the
      client, identified from client identity information the AS trusts,
      and presents goal and task_bounds as recorded, without truncation
      (Rendering Safeguards; #1532, D859).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Terminology defines "applicable
      entry" by the matching rule Audience and Token Response states,
      and adds the delegation terms; Mission State via Token
      Introspection describes a resource server that observes Mission
      state in plain terms; and the Implementation Map gains a table of
      deployment choices, naming for each who decides, which party
      learns it, and the existing signal, if any, it learns from (#1723,
      #1504, D845).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Mission-Bound Access Tokens
      opens with Issuance Checks, one list of the checks issuance
      applies, each with its home sections and its applicability and
      exceptions on code redemption, refresh, designation, delegated
      exchange, and self-exchange, and each of those paths points to the
      list; the Implementation Map gains an operation index naming the
      sections each operation needs (#1723, D845).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Policy-Language Types drops its
      Cedar example; Remediation Grains names its three grains in one
      paragraph; Grant Bindings and Related Mechanisms leaves the
      Template and Identity Continuation exchange mechanics to their
      profiles and merges the Mission Join paragraphs; Single
      Accountable Approver points to the Approval Governance Record for
      its mechanics, keeping both limitations; Approval Comprehension
      condenses its practices; and the Enforcement Responsibilities

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Token Claims, Delegated
      Exchange, Intent Submission Evidence, Revocation, Authority Hash
      and Mission Identity, and Authority Materialized Before
      Containment point to the home of a rule they restated, keeping
      each operation's own condition (#1793, D919).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Scope Projection is divided into
      subsections for the subset condition, the per-audience condition,
      the projection steps, requested scope values, scope-only targets,
      refresh-token protection, and its example; Intent Members lists a
      member's rules, the AS refusals included, under its definition,
      for each member but task_bounds; Principals and Approval Basis
      lists the rules that follow its member definitions; and Instance
      Context in Delegated Tokens lists its rules (#1723, D845).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Refresh Tokens, Designation,
      Direct Approval Steps, Preparing the Approval, Committing the
      Mission, Submission Processing Order, and Enforcement Requirements
      point to the rules of their operation that other sections state,
      which stay in place: the mission_intent exemption and the Mission-

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Declared Transformation
      Capabilities, Mission Lifecycle and Conformance drop restatements
      of rules their home sections state (#1793, D919).

   *  Four rules already stated as obligations carry BCP 14 keywords,
      with no new requirement: the current mapping does not stand in for
      an entry's original approved audience that the AS cannot establish
      (Declared Transformation Capabilities); the accountable principal
      is not inferred from the token sub (Authority Sources); on a
      refresh, the AS decides the Mission-governed resource refusal
      before it consumes or rotates the refresh token (Authority
      Proposal); and the issuer does not change a recorded termination
      (Mission Termination).  Editorial, no requirement change: Declared
      Transformation Capabilities lists the rules for an entry's
      original approved audience, the creation-replay rule joins the
      response list of Mission Identifier and Expiry in Responses, and
      Self-Exchange Down-Scoping points to its mission_intent exemption

   *  Two rules already stated as obligations carry BCP 14 keywords,
      with no new requirement: the AS refuses a token request outside a
      Mission any of whose audiences is Mission-governed, and issues no
      token for the others (Authority Proposal); and a Mission in a
      state from which it can return to active is not reported with a
      termination reason (Issuance Gating).  Editorial, no requirement
      change: Instance Context in Delegated Tokens names the grant
      lineage a delegated exchange establishes when it issues a refresh
      token (#1871, D1005).

   *  Mission Record: its members MUST NOT be changed after creation,
      except state and the termination member written once, with no new
      requirement; a companion's containment overlay or suspension
      schedule is issuer-held state associated with the Mission, outside
      the record (#1873, #1879, D1014).

   *  Editorial correction, no requirement change: Authority Sources
      says the Mission Record's client_id names the Agent and a token's

      states, so it no longer reads as saying a delegated token's
      client_id names the Agent (#1851, D971).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: Issuance Gating opens with the

      unchanged.  The Issuance Checks index is removed: the procedure

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: text that restates rules stated

   *  Revocation: a Mission Issuer MUST accept, through an authenticated

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: delegation rules that sat in

      and the End-to-End Example's agent-identity stage, each stated
      elsewhere, are removed with the uncited SPIFFE client
      authentication reference (#1892, D1026).

   *  Editorial, no requirement change: the Role Mapping appendix moves
      to the Architecture as Approval Role Mapping, which Single
      Accountable Approver cites; the companions that cited it now cite
      Principals and Approval Basis, which defines the three roles
      (#1892, D1026).

   *  Self-exchange is an OPTIONAL capability, independent of
      Delegation: an AS that does not support it refuses a no-actor
      exchange with unsupported_grant_type when it lacks the Token
      Exchange grant type, and otherwise with invalid_request;
      advertising that grant type does not establish every exchange
      variant.  An acknowledged revocation is the AS's commit of the
      Mission's termination with reason revoked.  Security
      Considerations state the consequence of a policy that authorizes
      no party to request revocation (#1892, D1031).

   *  Declared Transformation Capabilities: a type's specification can
      define a value other than a boolean for a capability member, and a
      value the reader cannot validate declares that capability false
      (#1377, D1025).

   *  Submission Processing Order: a commitment to the rendered approval
      surface may omit each provenance element's verified_at, which the
      AS generates at each verification, and no other member (#1897,
      D1035).

   -01

   *Breaking wire changes*

   *  mission_intent carries the Mission Intent Submission envelope,
      intent plus an optional typed evidence array; a bare Intent is
      refused, and unsupported, invalid, or missing required evidence is
      refused with the new invalid_mission_intent_evidence error code.
      PAR carries it on the authorization-code path; other grants need a
      companion (#1202).  The authority proposal moves from the removed
      Intent member proposed_authority to the authorization_details
      parameter, committed by the new proposal_hash.
   *  Inside a signed Request Object pushed through PAR, the
      mission_intent claim is the Submission envelope as a JSON object,
      not its serialization; any other value is refused with
      invalid_request (#1503).
   *  Intent members, with no aliases (#636, #706, #117): resources is
      renamed target_resources and constraints task_bounds; controls is
      removed, its acr replaced by the acr_values and max_age parameters
      and its max_derivations by Derivation Limits'
      requested_derivation_limit. success_criteria is removed, and
      earlier Missions keep their recorded Intent. goal_lang is new
      (#534).  The top level admits only defined members, a Mission
      Intent Members registry assigns short names, and test vectors are
      regenerated.
   *  The baseline mission claim carries id, issuer, and an optional
      expires_at; authority_hash leaves the claim, and introspection can
      disclose it (#702).  The Mission Record replaces approver with
      approval_basis.consent_principal (#705) and adds approval_basis,
      authority_source (#316), termination, proposed_authority,
      proposal_hash, and submission_evidence.
   *  The lifecycle states are active and terminated; the -00 states
      revoked and expired become termination reasons, and a consumer can
      read a legacy state that way during a transition (#705).  New
      registries hold lifecycle states, any further one non-terminal
      (#170), and termination reasons.
   *  The token endpoint adds mission_expires_at, the effective expiry,
      which can precede the requested intent.expires_at, and the
      mission_error diagnostic; mission_id is recommended.
      Authorization details type metadata can declare
      mission_transformation_capabilities, and entry commitments use
      mission-authority-entry.  Resource server challenges add
      mission_denial and use RFC 9470 insufficient_user_authentication.
   *  A Token Exchange refuses an inactive Mission (#1154) and an
      invalid, unproven, or unacceptable subject or actor token (#1178)
      with invalid_request, unless its profile assigns a code; other
      grants keep invalid_grant, and DPoP proof failures use
      invalid_dpop_proof and use_dpop_nonce. access_denied replaces
      invalid_authorization_details where no authorization_details was
      submitted; invalid_target covers scope-projection and routing
      failures; and invalid_scope covers an ungrantable scope and an
      openid request whose Approver is not the Subject (#826).
   *  Delegation by designation (#1268): the delegate's subject_token is
      a designation, which only the holder of a sender-constrained token
      obtains, by Token Exchange naming the delegate as audience.
   *  Instance context no longer identifies the instance through act.sub
      or act.cnf: the delegate's actor identity is established
      separately, the top-level cnf binds the presenting key, and a
      deployment can convey optional client_instance context under
      [I-D.draft-mcguinness-oauth-client-instance-id].  The instance-
      delegate matcher example uses the ai_agent actor type instead of
      the retired client_instance actor type.

   *Model changes.* Approval separates consent_principal,
   activation_actor, and optional adjudication (#701): direct approval
   is human (#1185), approval activates only authority its source holds
   (#316), and standing-consent bases activate instances at their own
   approval events (#1078).  Derivation is mechanical. purpose selects
   configured mappings; its other uses, and model output, only refuse or
   narrow (#1102).  Each grant lineage binds to one Mission, never
   through consent reuse (#700, #1259).  Authority is type-agnostic,
   with scope projection; opaque tokens use introspection or issuer-
   local resolution (#1203).  Submission processing checks a proposal
   before evidence, refuses with the earliest failing step's error, and
   completes Authority Set derivation before the rendering (#1411); the
   Mission-governed client rule reaches only authorization requests
   (#1448); delegation policy crosses a cross-domain projection where
   that profile's provenance rule keeps it (#1374).  An unrecognized
   authority_source.type stays opaque provenance and does not deactivate
   the Mission, though no Mission is approved from a source the AS does
   not recognize, and an authority_source.policy digest follows the
   construction and issuer its policy format defines (#1503).

   *Moved to companions.* Resource Access (the mission_resource_access
   type, Common Constraints, tool modeling, and
   mission_constraints_supported; #637, #645, #698); Intent Submission
   Evidence (the verification framework; evidence stays); Approved-Set
   Verification; Derivation Limits (with derivations_remaining);
   Metering's Intent members; and Approval Governance's assertion rules.

   *Security and privacy.* Delegation hardening and no authority from an
   entry a resource server cannot fully enforce (#1258); Approver
   authentication kept off derived tokens and out of OpenID Connect
   results for another Subject (#826); task credentials that are
   Mission-bound or covered by a trusted enforcement path (#1205);
   refresh tokens bound per client type (#1204); issuer-checked Mission
   resolution (#725); third-party data subjects (#344); key retention to
   the audit horizon; a resource server accepts a mission claim only
   from an issuer it trusts for Mission-bound authorization, with
   mission.issuer equal to iss outside an adopted cross-domain profile;
   the approval rendering identifies the receiving client from
   information the AS trusts; a refresh request beyond its grant is
   refused, never trimmed (#1503); and the approval basis named as
   outside the integrity anchors, with the profiles that commit it
   (#1518).

   *Editorial and structure.* The reading path (#910, #1379);
   readability, placement and consolidation passes (#876, #1409, #1447,
   #1472, #1479, #1483, #1490, #1501, #1526), with the issuance rules
   gathered under Mission-Bound Access Tokens, "Mission Lifecycle and
   Gating" retitled "Mission Lifecycle", resource classification named
   "Mission-governed resource" (#1503), and one Standing-Consent Bases
   section; the error-table detail for a configured-mapping refusal
   aligned with Derivation Modes (#1495); subsections, tables and IANA
   templates fitted to the text rendering (#1380, #1382, #1401, #1404,
   #1410, #1418); canonical-Mission examples (#1176); and the Mapping
   Assessment, moved from the substrate (#708).

   *References.* The WIMSE AIMS draft, the RAR metadata remediation
   draft, and Client Instance ID with Client Attesters replace draft-
   klrc-aiagent-auth, draft-zehavi-oauth-rar-metadata, and draft-
   mcguinness-oauth-client-instance-assertion; MCP, ISO 4217, RFC 3986,
   and draft-mcguinness-oauth-ai-agent-instance are removed; RFC 6750,
   RFC 9700, Client Instance ID, OpenID Connect Core, the RAR metadata
   remediation draft and, for Delegation, the OAuth Actor Profile are
   normative.

   -00

   *  Initial individual draft.

Acknowledgments

   This work builds on the OAuth 2.0 Rich Authorization Requests, Pushed
   Authorization Requests, and JWT access token specifications, and is
   intended to complement agent-identity work including
   [I-D.draft-ietf-wimse-aims].
